The unchanged v6 retry completes all twelve processes at 14 and 22 GB, with every physical ceiling and natural-answer completion check passing. Its frozen analysis excludes cell 6 for thermal state and cell 9 for sustained competing CPU plus global paging. The complete attempt remains timing-discarded; no selected subset, clean median or speed qualification is reported.

The recorded process inspection identifies the active Codex renderer among cell 9's competing PIDs. The app window was subsequently hidden through the ordinary macOS visibility control to reduce UI work. This does not prove continuous host isolation. No browser tab was closed, and the recovered Bluetooth service remains idle in the attached snapshot.

The existing runner now requires 120 continuously quiet nominal seconds before each process, replacing 20. Its five-minute admission deadline and all in-process timing, memory, pressure, CPU, paging and storage exclusions remain unchanged. All twenty runner tests pass, including the longer continuously quiet interval and its reset. This conservative cooling heuristic costs additional benchmark time and targets controlled interactive bursts; it does not establish sustained-session thermal performance. The native binary, recipes, task inputs, three paired rounds and 14/22 GB ceilings are unchanged.

The prospective v7 protocol is b45b90482fe6c66244a1226f42461a87568a9e1411e0e3649bac39c8b7021c7b. Its one-shot preflight observes a renderer spike and launches no model. The campaign parent is then started under its own stricter continuous admission wait; no one-shot observation overrides that guard. Its result is pending and is not included here. No partial throughput outcome informed the cooling change. Public permission and alternative product activation remain unresolved.

Each following block contains zlib-compressed exact bytes, their original size and SHA-256. The preceding protocol and binary closure remain in [[sources/runs/2026/10/2026-10-05-practical-benchmark-host-recovery]].
